<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:intro-offline}
Let $O$ be an optimal solution and $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$.  For every
monotone $\gamma$-weakly submodular objective $f$, our proposed \textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ algorithm will output a 
randomized feasible base $S_{\mathrm{out}}$ satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=2.2em]
\item under a general matroid $\mathcal{M}=(U,\mathcal{I})$,
\begin{equation}\label{intro_add}
   \mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]
 \ge (\rho_\gamma-\varepsilon)f(O), 
\end{equation}where where $\rho_\gamma\triangleq1-\left(\gamma/(2-\gamma)\right)^{\gamma^2/(2(1-\gamma))}$ when $\gamma\in(0,1)$ and $\rho_1=1-1/e$;
\item under a $k$-size cardinality constraint, $\mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]
 \ge \bigl(1-e^{-\gamma}-\epsilon\bigr)f(O)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:intro-stronger}
Let $O$ be an optimal base of a general matroid $\mathcal{M}=(U,\mathcal{I})$ and fix
$\varepsilon\in(0,1)$.  There exists a randomized algorithm that returns a
base $S_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathcal{I}$ satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=2.2em]
\item for every normalized monotone $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular
objective $f$,
\[
 \mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]
 \ge (R_{\gamma,\beta}-\varepsilon)f(O),
\]
where $R_{\gamma,\beta}\triangleq1-\left(\zeta/(2-\zeta)\right)^{\gamma\zeta/(2(1-\zeta))}$ and $\zeta=\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$;
\item for every normalized monotone $\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular objective $f$,
\[
 \mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]
 \ge (1-e^{-\alpha}-\varepsilon)f(O).
\]
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:intro-online}
Fix $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, let $k$ be the rank of a general matroid $\mathcal{M}=(U,\mathcal{I})$  and
$n=|U|$, if we consider an oblivious sequence of normalized monotone
objectives, namely, $\{f_1,\dots, f_T\}$,
then there exists a randomized online algorithm satisfies
\begin{align*}
 \operatorname{Reg}_{\rho_\gamma-\varepsilon}(T)
 &=O\bigl(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\bigr)
 &&\text{for $\gamma$-weakly submodular maximization under a matroid},\\
 \operatorname{Reg}_{1-e^{-\gamma(1-\varepsilon)}}(T)
 &=O\bigl(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\bigr)
 &&\text{for $\gamma$-weakly submodular maximization under cardinality},\\
 \operatorname{Reg}_{R_{\gamma,\beta}-\varepsilon}(T)
 &=O\bigl(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\bigr)
 &&\text{for $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular maximization},\\
 \operatorname{Reg}_{1-e^{-\alpha}-\varepsilon}(T)
 &=O\bigl(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\bigr)
 &&\text{for $\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular maximization},
\end{align*} where the symbol $ \operatorname{Reg}_\rho(T)$ denotes the $\rho$-regret of \citet{chen2018projection,chen2018online} and $\rho\in(0,1]$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
For a positive integer $m$, we let $[m]=\{1,\ldots,m\}$ and suppose that the finite ground set
is $U$, with $n=|U|$.  For any $A\subseteq U$ and $e\in U$, we write
$A+e=A\cup\{e\}$ and let $\1_A\in\{0,1\}^U$ be
the indicator vector of $A$.  Moreover, the symbols $\langle x,y\rangle$ and $x\mathbin{\odot} y$ denote the
Euclidean inner product and coordinate-wise product, respectively.  For a set function $f:2^U\to\mathbb{R}_+$, we define
$f(B\mid A)=f(A\cup B)-f(A)$. Unless stated otherwise, in this paper, $f$ is \emph{normalized} with  $f(\varnothing)=0$, and \emph{monotone}, namely,
$f(A)\le f(B)$ for $A\subseteq B\subseteq U$.
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
A monotone set function $f$ is $\gamma$-weakly submodular, if for any $A\subseteq B\subseteq U$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weak-submod}
 \sum_{e\in B\setminus A}f(e\mid A)\ge \gamma\bigl(f(B)-f(A)\bigr).
\end{equation}
The parameter $\gamma\in(0,1]$ is called submodularity ratio~\citep{das2011submodular,das2018approximate}.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
A monotone set function $f$ is \emph{$\beta$-weakly submodular from above}, if for any $A\subseteq B\subseteq U$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weak-upper}
 \sum_{e\in B\setminus A}f(e\mid B-e)
 \le \beta\bigl(f(B)-f(A)\bigr).
\end{equation}
The parameter $\beta\ge1$ is called upper submodularity ratio. Moreover, we say that a function is \((\gamma,\beta)\)-weakly submodular if it satisfies both \eqref{eq:weak-submod} and \eqref{eq:weak-upper}~\citep{thiery2022two,zhang2026multinoulli}.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:weak-dr}
A monotone set function $f$ is $\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular, if for any $A\subseteq B\subseteq U$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weak-dr}
 f(e\mid A)\ge \alpha f(e\mid B).
\end{equation}
The parameter $\alpha\in(0,1]$ is the diminishing-returns(DR) ratio~\citep{lu2022maximizing,zhang2026multinoulli}.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:brualdi-map}
For every pair of bases $S,O\in\mathcal{B}$, there exists a bijection
$h_{S,O}:S\to O$ such that
\begin{equation*}
    S-i+h_{S,O}(i)\in\mathcal{B},\quad\text{for any}\ \ i\in S.
\end{equation*}Moreover, the bijection can be chosen so that
$h_{S,O}(i)=i$ for every $i\in S\cap O$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:conditioned-generator}
For every $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1)$ and $S\in\mathcal{B}$, the following identity holds:
\begin{align}
  \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 =\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^*(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i}),
\end{align} where $j_i^*(S,\tau)\in\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{j\in\mathcal C_S(i)}
\nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})$. Thus, the unconditional potential function satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Q-derivative-average}
 \left.\frac{\partial^+Q(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}=\sum_{S\in\mathcal{B}}\Pr[A(\tau)=S]
 \cdot\left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,t}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}=\mathbb{E}_{A(\tau)}\left[\sum_{i\in A(\tau)}\nabla_{j_i^*(A(\tau),\tau)}F(\tau\1_{A(\tau)-i})\right],
\end{equation} where $A(\tau)$ is the random base at time $\tau$  of Algorithm~ 
    \ifcsname r@alg:mgpe\endcsname

<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:repair}When $f$ is monotone $\gamma$-weakly submodular, for any $i\in S$ and $j\in U$, we can show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:repair}
 \nabla_{j} F(\tau\1_{S-i})\ge \frac{\gamma}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]
 -\frac{(1-\gamma)\tau}{C_\gamma(\tau)}
   \nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i}),
\end{equation} where $R$ is the random subset drawn from the point $\tau\1_{S}$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:gamma-drift}
For every $S\in\mathcal{B}$ and time $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]$, when $f$ is $\gamma$-weakly submodular, the conditioned value process  $V_{S,\tau}(r)$ and potential function $Q(r)$ of \textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ satisfies that
\begin{align}
   \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial \tau}\right|_{r=\tau}
 &\ge \frac{\gamma^2}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}
 \bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr) \label{eq:gamma-drift1}\\
 \left.\frac{\partial^+Q(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&\ge\frac{\gamma^2}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\label{eq:gamma-drift2}
 \bigl(f(O)-Q(\tau)\bigr)
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->

<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:strict-barrier}
For $\gamma\in(0,1]$, let
$s_\gamma\triangleq\gamma(2+3\gamma-\gamma^2)/(2(\gamma+2))$. Then, for any 
$0<\delta<(1-s_\gamma)/2$, there exist
 a monotone $\gamma$-weakly submodular and a rank-two partition matroid such
that the return subset of \textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\  satisfies
\[ \frac{\mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]}{f(O)}=s_\gamma+2\delta,\]where \(O\) is an optimal base. Particularly when  $0<\gamma<0.8576$, $s_\gamma<1-e^{-\gamma}$. Consequently, if we choose $0<\delta<\frac{1-e^{-\gamma}-s_\gamma}{2}$, then $\mathbb{E}[f(S_{\mathrm{out}})]/f(O)<1-e^{-\gamma}$.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->

\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ for matroid-constrained $\gamma$-weakly submodular maximization problems.

$R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)\le1.01\rho_\gamma$ for
$\gamma\in\{0.5,0.7,0.8,0.9\}$,
$R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)\le1.05\rho_\gamma$ for
$\gamma\in\{0.3,0.4\}$, and
$R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)\le1.14\rho_\gamma$ for
$\gamma=0.2$. Consequently, for six of the seven parameter values
reported above, the guarantee $\rho_\gamma$ is tight up to a
multiplicative gap of at most $5\%$; for four of these values, the gap
is below $1\%$.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:repair1}
Let $S$ be a base and $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]$. Then, when $f$ is monotone $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular, we can show that
\begin{align}
   \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 &\ge \frac{\gamma\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}
    \bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr)\label{eq:gamma-drift1+}\\
 \left.\frac{\partial^+Q(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&\ge\frac{\gamma\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\label{eq:gamma-drift2+}
 \bigl(f(O)-Q(\tau)\bigr),
\end{align} where  $\zeta \coloneqq \max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$. Furthermore, if $f$ is monotone $\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular, we also have
\begin{align}
   \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,t}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 &\ge \alpha
    \bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr)\label{eq:gamma-drift1++}\\
 \left.\frac{\partial^+Q(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&\ge\alpha\label{eq:gamma-drift2++}
 \bigl(f(O)-Q(\tau)\bigr),
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->

    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-thm:offline3\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-thm:offline3} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-thm:offline3} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi implies that the additional structure encoded by
both weak DR-submodularity and upper weak submodularity can yield stronger guarantees than the generic
$\rho_\gamma$ bound. Specifically, for $\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular
objectives, the resulting factor
$1-e^{-\alpha}$ strictly exceeds $\rho_\gamma$ when $\gamma\leq\alpha$. Similarly, for $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular functions,
$R_{\gamma,\beta}$ also strictly improves upon $\rho_\gamma$ when
$\beta<2-\gamma$, while recovering $\rho_\gamma$ when
$\beta\geq2-\gamma$.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:balanced}
Fix a base $S\in\mathcal{B}$ and $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$.  A fractional exchange matrix from $S$ with insertion marginal $\boldsymbol{x}$ is a nonnegative matrix $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}\in\mathbb{R}_+^{S\times U}$ satisfying
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]\emph{Feasible support:} $ Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)>0\Longrightarrow S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}$;
\item[(ii)]\emph{Row capacity:} $\sum_{j\in U}Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\le 1$, for every $i\in S$;
   
 \item[(iii)]\emph{Insertion marginals:} $\sum_{i\in S}Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)=x_j$,  for every $j\in U$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-4 -->

    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-def:balanced} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-def:balanced\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-def:balanced} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-def:balanced} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi differs slightly in form from the
fractional exchange matrix introduced by
\citet{wan2026banditsubmodularmaximizationmatroid}. Specifically, condition~\textnormal{(ii)} imposes only a row-capacity constraint,
whereas \citet{wan2026banditsubmodularmaximizationmatroid} require every
row sum to be exactly one. This relaxation allows us to define a
fractional exchange matrix for every point $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$, including
points satisfying $\sum_{i}x_i<k$, while their definition is restricted to
full-rank points, i.e., $\sum_{i}x_i=k$. In particular, when $\sum_{i}x_i\equiv k$, we will show that our definition recovers that of
\citet{wan2026banditsubmodularmaximizationmatroid}.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:balanced}
For every $S\in\mathcal{B}$ and $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$, a fractional exchange matrix $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}$ exists.  Given a $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$, one can compute $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}$ via a feasible transportation flow with $k+n$ nonterminal vertices and at most $kn$ exchange arcs.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:fractional-exchange-properties}
Fix a base $S\in\mathcal{B}$ and a fractional point $\boldsymbol{x}\triangleq(x_1,\dots,x_{|U|})\in P(\mathcal{M})$, the following properties hold:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item[(i)] If $\boldsymbol{x}$ is full-rank, namely, $\sum_{i\in U}x_i=k$, $\sum_{j\in U}Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)=1$ for every $i\in S$;
   \item[(ii)] If $\boldsymbol{x}=\1_S$, then  $Q_S^{\1_S}(i,j)=\1\{i=j\}$ for every $i\in S$ and $j\in U$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:conditioned-generator1}
Fix an online round $t$, a base $S\in\mathcal{B}$, and a full-rank fractional point
$\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$. Let $f_t:2^U\to[0,1]$ be a set function with
multilinear extension $F_t$. Consider the Poisson exchange process
$\{A_t(r)\}_{r\in[\varepsilon,1]}$ with intensity $ \lambda(\tau)=\frac{k}{\tau}$, where each exchange pair is
drawn according to $(i,j)\sim\frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)}{k}$.Then, for every $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fractional-generator1}
    \left.
    \frac{\partial^+V^t_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}
    \right|_{r=\tau}
    =\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}
    Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr),
\end{equation} where $V^t_{S,\tau}(r)=
    \mathbb{E}\left[
        F_t\bigl(r\1_{A_t(r)}\bigr)
        \,\middle|\,
        A_t(\tau)=S
    \right]$ is the induced conditioned value process.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{add_important} For any $S,O\in\mathcal{B}$ and $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]$, when $f_t$ is $\gamma$-weakly submodular, if we set $a_{\gamma}(\tau)\triangleq\frac{\gamma+(1-\gamma)\tau}
        {\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\in[\frac{1}{2},1]$ and $\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\triangleq(\1-\tau\1_S)\mathbin{\odot}\nabla F_t(\tau\1_S)$, then, the following inequality holds:
   \begin{equation}\label{add_15_1}
\begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\gamma}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)+(1-a_{\gamma}(\tau))Q_S^{\1_{S}}(i,j)\right)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S}))+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle.       
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->

\label{ass:ope-oracles}

the weight oracle $\mathcal{A}(r)$ in Algorithm~

    \fi

      \ref{main-main-main-alg:ope} 
    \fi 
    \fi

    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:ope\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:ope\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-alg:ope} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:ope} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-alg:ope\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:ope} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-alg:ope} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi satisfy the
following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item[(i)]At round $t$, the oracle $\mathcal{E}(r)$ outputs a \textbf{full-rank}
    $\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r}\in P(\mathcal{M})$ and subsequently receives the unbiased estimator $\tilde{\mathbf{g}}_{t,S_{t,r-1},\tau_r}\in[0,1]^n$. Then, for every horizon
    $T$ and any comparator $\mathbf u\in P(\mathcal{M})$, we have
    \[
        \mathbb{E}\left[
            \sum_{t=1}^T
            \left\langle
                \mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r},
                \mathbf{g}_{t,S_{t,r-1},\tau_r}
            \right\rangle
        \right]
        \leq O\left(
            k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\right).
    \]
   
    \item[(ii)]
    At round $t$, the oracle $\mathcal{A}(r)$ outputs
    $a_{t,r}\in[1/2,1]$ and subsequently receives the unbiased scalar estimator $\tilde{\Delta}_{t,r}\in[-k,k]$. Then, for every horizon
    $T$ and any comparator $a\in[1/2,1]$,
    \[
        \mathbb{E}\left[
            \sum_{t=1}^T
            \bigl(a-a_{t,r}\bigr)\Delta_{t,r}
        \right]
        \leq O(k\sqrt{T}),
    \] where the definition of $\Delta_{t,r}$ comes from Eq.\eqref{add_delta}.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end assumption-1 -->

<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{add_important1}
For any $S,O\in\mathcal{B}$, $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]$, and any full-rank
fractional point $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$, suppose that $f_t$ is monotone and
$(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular. Let
$\zeta\coloneqq\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$, $a_\zeta(\tau)=
    \frac{\zeta+(1-\zeta)\tau}
         {\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}
    \in(1/2,1]$, and define $\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}=
    (\1-\tau\1_S)\mathbin{\odot}\nabla F_t(\tau\1_S)$.
Then the following inequality holds:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:online-two-sided-fractional-drift}
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}
    \left(a_{\zeta}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
        +\bigl(1-a_{\zeta}(\tau)\bigr)Q_S^{\1_S}(i,j)
    \right)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})                                    \\&\geq
    \frac{\gamma\zeta}
         {\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}
    \bigl(f_t(O)-F_t(\tau\1_S)\bigr)
    +
    \frac{\zeta}
         {\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}
    \left\langle
        \boldsymbol{x}-\1_O,\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}
    \right\rangle .
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->

\end{equation} where $R_{\gamma,\beta}\triangleq1-\left(\zeta/(2-\zeta)\right)^{\gamma\zeta/(2(1-\zeta))}$ and $\zeta=\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$.

<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:32} Fix any \(S,O\in\mathcal{B}\), \(\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]\), and \(\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})\), where \(\mathcal{M}\) is the rank-\(k\) uniform matroid. Suppose that \(f_t\) is monotone \(\gamma\)-weakly submodular. For every \(j\in U\), define \begin{equation*}\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}(j)\triangleq\begin{cases}\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i}),& j\notin S\\[.5mm]\quad \nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-j})& j\in S\end{cases}\end{equation*}Then, we can prove that

\begin{equation}\label{eq:online-card-drift}\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\bar Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\ge\gamma\bigl(f_t(O)-F_t(\tau\1_S)\bigr)+\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_O,\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle.\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->

<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:online-alpha-drift}
Fix any $S,O\in\mathcal{B}$, $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1]$, and
any positive full-rank $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M}))$. Suppose that $f_t$ is monotone and
$\alpha$-weakly DR-submodular. For each $j\in U$, we define
\begin{equation}\label{equ:ell}
\begin{aligned}
\ell_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}(j)\triangleq
\sum_{i\in S}
\frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)}{x_j}
\nabla_j F_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}Then, we can have that 
\begin{align}
\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}
Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
\nabla_j F_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)
\ge\alpha\bigl(
f_t(O)-F_t(\tau\1_S)
\bigr)+
\left\langle
\boldsymbol{x}-\1_O,\ell_{t,S,\tau,\mathbf{y}}
\right\rangle.
\label{eq:online-alpha-fractional-drift}
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->

<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:potential-comparison}
Let both \(V\in\mathbb R\) and \(\delta\ge0\) be constants, and let the function \(\eta:[a,b]\to\mathbb R_+\) be continuous. If the function \(q:[a,b]\to\mathbb R\) satisfies the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=18pt]
    \item \(q\) is continuous on \((a,b)\);
    \item \(q\) is right-continuous at \(a\) and left-continuous at \(b\);
    \item \(q'_+(\tau)\) exists for every \(\tau\in[a,b)\);
    \item for any $\tau\in[a,b)$, the following inequality holds, namely,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:potential-local-drift}
q'_+(\tau)
\ge
\eta(\tau)\bigl(V-q(\tau)\bigr)-\delta
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}, then  we can have, for every \(\tau\in[a,b]\),
\begin{align}
q(\tau)\ge
e^{-H(\tau)}q(a)
+\bigl(1-e^{-H(\tau)}\bigr)V-\delta\int_a^\tau
\exp\left(-\int_s^t\eta(u)\,du\right)ds,
\end{align} where  $H(\tau)\triangleq\int_a^\tau\eta(u)\,du$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We first construct a function \(z:[a,b]\to\mathbb R\) and suppose that it is
be the solution of 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:comparison-trajectory-ode}
z'(\tau)
=
\eta(\tau)\bigl(V-z(\tau)\bigr)-\delta,
\qquad
z(a)=q(a).
\end{equation}Consequently, we can show that 
\begin{align}
z(\tau)=e^{-H(\tau)}q(a)
+\bigl(1-e^{-H(\tau)}\bigr)V-\delta\int_a^\tau
\exp\left(-\int_s^\tau\eta(u)\,du\right)ds.
\label{eq:comparison-trajectory-explicit}
\end{align}
Next, we prove that \(q(\tau)\ge z(\tau)\) for every \(\tau\in[a,b]\).

We first consider the case in which
Eq.\eqref{eq:potential-local-drift} holds strictly, namely,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:potential-strict-local-drift}
q'_+(\tau)
>
\eta(\tau)\bigl(V-q(\tau)\bigr)-\delta
\qquad\text{for every }\tau\in[a,b).
\end{equation}
Fix an arbitrary \(\bar \tau\in(a,b]\) and define
\[
\mathcal X_{\bar \tau}
\triangleq
\{\tau\in[a,\bar \tau]:q(\tau)\ge z(\tau)\}.
\]
Since \(q(a)=z(a)\), the set \(\mathcal X_{\bar t}\) is nonempty. Let $s\triangleq\sup\mathcal X_{\bar \tau}$. If \(s=\bar \tau\), there exists a sequence
\(\{\tau_m\}_{m\ge1}\subseteq\mathcal X_{\bar \tau}\) converging to
\(\bar \tau\) from the left. The continuity assumptions on \(q\), together
with the continuity of \(z\), then imply $q(\bar \tau)
=
\lim_{m\to\infty}q(\tau_m)
\ge
\lim_{m\to\infty}z(\tau_m)
=
z(\bar \tau)$ such that 
\[
q(\tau)\ge z(\tau),\quad\text{for every}\,\tau\in[a,\bar \tau].
\]

As for the scenario that \(s<\bar \tau\), we also infer that \(q(s)= z(s)\). Like the previous analysis, by continuity, we can show \(q(s)\ge z(s)\). If \(q(s)>z(s)\), and right continuity would give
\(q(t)>z(t)\) for some \(t>s\), contradicting the definition of \(s\).
Thus, $q(s)=z(s)$, when \(s<\bar \tau\). Furthermore, because \(s=\sup\mathcal X_{\bar t}\), we have $q(s+h)<z(s+h)$ for every $h\in(0,\bar t-s]$. Then, we can show  that
\begin{align}
q'_+(s)
&=
\lim_{h\downarrow0}
\frac{q(s+h)-q(s)}{h}
\nonumber\\
&\le
\lim_{h\downarrow0}
\frac{z(s+h)-z(s)}{h}
=
z'(s).
\label{eq:first-contact-comparison}
\end{align}
On the other hand, by
Eq.\eqref{eq:potential-strict-local-drift},
\(q(s)=z(s)\), and Eq.\eqref{eq:comparison-trajectory-ode},
\[
q'_+(s)
>
\eta(s)\bigl(V-q(s)\bigr)-\delta
=
\eta(s)\bigl(V-z(s)\bigr)-\delta
=
z'(s),
\]
which contradicts Eq.\eqref{eq:first-contact-comparison}. Hence the case
\(s<\bar t\) is impossible, and therefore, for any fixed \(\bar \tau\in(a,b]\), we conclude
that
\[
q(\tau)\ge z(\tau),\quad\text{for every}\, \tau\in[a,\bar \tau].
\]

We now remove the strictness assumption of Eq.\eqref{eq:potential-strict-local-drift}. For every \(\xi>0\), define
\[
q_\xi(\tau)\triangleq q(\tau)+\xi(\tau-a).
\]
The function \(q_\xi\) satisfies the same continuity assumptions as \(q\),
and \(q_\xi(a)=q(a)=z(a)\). Moreover, for every \(\tau\in[a,b)\),
\begin{align}
(q_\xi)'_+(\tau)
&=q'_+(\tau)+\xi\ge
\eta(\tau)\bigl(V-q(\tau)\bigr)-\delta+\xi\\
&=
\eta(\tau)\bigl(V-q_\xi(\tau)\bigr)-\delta
+\xi\bigl(1+\eta(t)(\tau-a)\bigr)\\&>
\eta(t)\bigl(V-q_\xi(\tau)\bigr)-\delta,
\end{align}
where the final inequality follows from \(\eta(t)\ge0\). 

Therefore, we can show  $q_\xi(\tau)\ge z(\tau)$ for every  $\tau\in[a,b]$. Letting \(\xi\downarrow0\) yields \(q(\tau)\ge z(\tau)\). Combining this
comparison with Eq.\eqref{eq:comparison-trajectory-explicit} proves Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:potential-comparison\endcsname

<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:finite-start-correction}
Let \(0<a\le\theta\le1\) and \(\varepsilon\in[0,1]\). Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-start-function}
\Psi_{\theta,a}(s)
\triangleq\left(\frac{a+2(1-a)s}{2-a}
\right)^{\frac{\theta a}{2(1-a)}}\quad\forall s\in[0,1].
\end{equation}Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-start-general-bound}
\Psi_{\theta,a}(\varepsilon)
\le\Psi_{\theta,a}(0)+\varepsilon.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
For convenience, we define
\[
p_{\theta,a}
\triangleq
\frac{\theta a}{2(1-a)}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
x_a(s)
\triangleq
\frac{a+2(1-a)s}{2-a}.
\]
Thus, we can rewrite $\Psi_{\theta,a}(s)=x_a(s)^{p_{\theta,a}}$. Differentiating \(\Psi_{\theta,a}\) gives
\begin{align}
\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)=
p_{\theta,a}\cdot x_a(s)^{p_{\theta,a}-1}
\cdot \frac{2(1-a)}{2-a}=
\frac{\theta a}{2-a}
x_a(s)^{p_{\theta,a}-1}.
\label{eq:finite-start-derivative}
\end{align}Moreover, for every \(s\in[0,1]\), we know that  $\frac{a}{2-a}
\le
x_a(s)
\le1$.

Next, we prove that $\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)\le1$ for any $s\in[0,1]$ by distinguishing two cases.

Firstly, we suppose that \(p_{\theta,a}\ge1\). Since \(x_a(s)\le1\),
we have \(x_a(s)^{p_{\theta,a}-1}\le1\). Hence,
\[
\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)
\le
\frac{\theta a}{2-a}
\le1,
\]
where the final inequality follows from
\(0<\theta\le1\) and \(0<a\le1\).

As for \(p_{\theta,a}<1\), due to \(x_a(s)\ge a/(2-a)\), we ca  obtain
\begin{align*}
\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)
&=\frac{\theta a}{2-a}
x_a(s)^{p_{\theta,a}-1}\le
\frac{\theta a}{2-a}
\left(\frac{a}{2-a}\right)^{p_{\theta,a}-1}\\
&=
\theta
\left(\frac{a}{2-a}\right)^{p_{\theta,a}}
\le1.
\end{align*} Therefore, we have $\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)\le1$ for any $s\in[0,1]$. Integrating this derivative bound over \([0,\varepsilon]\) gives
\[
\Psi_{\theta,a}(\varepsilon)-\Psi_{\theta,a}(0)
=
\int_0^\varepsilon
\Psi_{\theta,a}'(s)\,ds
\le
\varepsilon.
\]
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:transition-derivative}
For any $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1)$ and any $S,T\in\mathcal{B}$, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transition-derivative}
 \left.\frac{\partial_+\Pr[A(r)=T\mid A(\tau)=S]}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 =
 \begin{cases}
 \lambda(\tau)p_{i,j}(\tau,S), & T=S-i+j,\ j\notin S,\\
 -\lambda(\tau)\left(1-\sum_{i\in S}p_{i,i}(\tau,S)\right), & T=S,\\
 0, & \text{otherwise},
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}where \(p_{i,j}(\tau,S)\) denotes the probability that we delete
\(i\) and insert \(j\), conditional on a Poisson event occurring at time
\(\tau\) while the current base is \(S\).
<!-- end lemma-13 -->

<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:core_lemma+}
For every $\tau\in[\varepsilon,1)$ and $S\in\mathcal{B}$,
\begin{align}
 \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_S)
  +\lambda(t)\mathbb{E}_{(i,j)\sim p(\tau,S)}
    \left[F(\tau\1_{S-i+j})-F(\tau\1_S)\right]\label{eq:generator-general}\\
 &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_S)
  +\tau\lambda(\tau)\mathbb{E}_{(i,j)\sim p(\tau,S)}
  \left[\nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})-\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i})\right].\notag
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
According to the definition of $V_{S,\tau}(r)$, we can show that
\begin{equation*}
    V_{S,\tau}(r)\triangleq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{B}}F(r\1_{T})\cdot \Pr[A(r)=T\mid A(\tau)=S].
\end{equation*}
Then, we have that 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
    &\left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\\&=\sum_{T\in\mathcal{B}}\left.\frac{\partial^+F(r\1_{T})}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\cdot \Pr[A(\tau)=T\mid A(\tau)=S]+ \sum_{T\in\mathcal{B}}\left.F(\tau\1_{T})\frac{\partial^+\Pr[A(r)=T\mid A(\tau)=S]}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\\
    &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j}F(\tau\1_{S})-\sum_{i\in S}\lambda(\tau)\left(1-\sum_{i\in S}p_{i,i}(\tau,S)\right)\cdot F(\tau \1_{S})+\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j:S-i+j\in\mathcal{B},j\notin S}\lambda(\tau)p_{i,j}(\tau,S)\cdot F(\tau \1_{S-i+j}),\\
    &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j}F(\tau\1_{S})-\sum_{i\in S}\lambda(\tau)\left(1-\sum_{i\in S}p_{i,i}(\tau,S)\right)\cdot F(\tau \1_{S})+\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U\setminus S}\lambda(\tau)p_{i,j}(\tau,S)\cdot F(\tau \1_{S-i+j})\\
    &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j}F(\tau\1_{S})+\lambda(\tau)\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}p_{i,j}(\tau,S)\cdot \big(F(\tau \1_{S-i+j})-F(\tau\1_{S})\big)\\&=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_S)
  +\lambda(t)\mathbb{E}_{(i,j)\sim p(\tau,S)}
    \left[F(\tau\1_{S-i+j})-F(\tau\1_S)\right],
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the second equality follows from  Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:transition-derivative\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:transition-derivative\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:transition-derivative\endcsname

    \fi

      \ref{main-main-main-lem:transition-derivative} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:transition-derivative} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi; the third equality comes from $p_{i,j}(\tau,S)\equiv0$ if $S-i+j\notin\mathcal{B}$ and the final  equality comes from $\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}p_{i,j}=1$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
If $j=i$, we can show that 
\begin{equation*}
    \mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]=\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(i\mid R)\right]=(1-\tau)\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S-i}}\left[f(i\mid R)\right]=(1-\tau)\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i}).
\end{equation*}where the second equation comes from $f(i\mid R)=0$ when $i\in R$. Hence, if $j=i$, we have that
\begin{equation*}
   \begin{aligned}
\frac{\gamma}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]
 -\frac{(1-\gamma)\tau}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i})\le\frac{\gamma}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]=\frac{\gamma(1-\tau)}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i}).
   \end{aligned} 
\end{equation*}Due to that $\gamma(1-\tau)\le C_\gamma(\tau)=\gamma+(1-\gamma)\tau$, we thus have Eq.\eqref{eq:repair}.


Now suppose $j\notin S$ and set $R_0\sim \tau\1_{S-i}$. At first, we have
\begin{equation}\label{equ:add1}
    \mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]=(1-\tau)\mathbb{E} _{R_0}\left[f(j\mid R_0)\right]+\tau\mathbb{E} _{R_0}\left[f(j\mid R_0+i)\right].
\end{equation}Then, applying weak submodularity with
$A=R_0$ and $B=R_0+i+j$ gives
\begin{equation*}\label{eq:repair-two-point}
f(i\mid R_0)+f(j\mid R_0)\ge\gamma\left(f(R_0+i+j)-f(R_0)\right)=\gamma\left(f(j\mid R_0+i)+f(i\mid R_0)\right).
\end{equation*} 
As a result, we get the following inequality:
\begin{equation}\label{equ:add2}
\begin{aligned}
\nabla_{j} F(t\1_{S-i})=\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(j\mid R_0)]\ge\gamma\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(j\mid R_0+i)]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(i\mid R_0)]
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Multiplying the Eq.\eqref{equ:add2} by $t$, we then have that 
\begin{equation}\label{equ:add3}
    \begin{aligned}
        \tau\nabla_{j} F(\tau\1_{S-i})&=\tau\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(j\mid R_0)]\ge\gamma(\tau\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(j\mid R_0+i)])-(1-\gamma)\tau\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(i\mid R_0)]\\
        &=\gamma(E _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]-(1-\tau)\mathbb{E} _{R_0}\left[f(j\mid R_0)\right])-(1-\gamma)\tau\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(i\mid R_0)]\\
        &=\gamma E _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(j\mid R)\right]-(1-\tau)\gamma\nabla_{j} F(\tau\1_{S-i})-(1-\gamma)\tau\mathbb{E}_{R_0}[f(i\mid R_0)],
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation} where the second equation comes from  Eq.\eqref{equ:add1}. Therefore, adding $(1-\tau)\gamma\nabla_{j} F(\tau\1_{S-i})$ to both sides of Eq.\eqref{equ:add3} and then dividing by  $C_{\gamma}(\tau)$, we obtain Eq.\eqref{eq:repair}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $O$ be the optimal solution and $h:S\to O$ be a Brualdi map. Since $h(i)\in\mathcal C_S(i)$, Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:repair\endcsname

      \ref{main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:repair\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-lem:repair\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:repair} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:repair} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi gives that
\begin{equation}\label{add:4}
    \begin{aligned}
        &\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^{*}(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})\ge\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{h(i)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&\ge\frac{\gamma}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[\sum_{i\in S}f(h(i)\mid R)\right]
 -\frac{(1-\gamma)\tau}{C_\gamma(\tau)}
   \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i})\\
   &\ge\frac{\gamma^{2}}{C_\gamma(\tau)}\big(f(O)-\mathbb{E} _{R\sim \tau\1_{S}}\left[f(R)\right]\big)
 -\frac{(1-\gamma)\tau}{C_\gamma(\tau)}
   \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i}),
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation} where the second inequality comes from the $\gamma$-weak submodularity, that is, $\sum_{i\in S}f(h(i)\mid R)\ge\gamma(f(O)-f(R))$. Furthermore, due to $i\in\mathcal C_S(i)$, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:self-absorption}
\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^{*}(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})\ge \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i}).
\end{equation}
Replacing $\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i})$ in Eq.\eqref{add:4} by $\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^{*}(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})$ and moving the resulting term to the left yields
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^{*}(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})
 \ge\frac{\gamma^2}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr).
\end{equation*}
Merging this inequality into the result of Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
      \ref{lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \else
      \ref{main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \fi

\end{equation*}
Then, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}

         &\ge(1-e^{-H(1)})f(O)=\left(1-(\frac{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\epsilon}{2-\gamma})^{\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2(1-\gamma)}}\right)f(O)\ge(1-\rho_{\gamma}-\epsilon)f(O),
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the second inequality comes from $Q(\epsilon)\ge0$ and the final inequality comes from Lemma~

<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Fix a base $S$, an optimal base $O\in\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{|S|\le k}f(S)$, and $t\in(\varepsilon,1]$, we define 
\begin{equation*}
 X=O\setminus S,\qquad Y=O\cap S,\qquad Z=S\setminus O,
 \qquad \ell=|X|=|Z|,
\end{equation*}
and abbreviate the maximum-gain sum by
\begin{equation*}
 G_S(\tau)=\sum_{i\in S}\max_{j:\,S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}}
             \nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i}),
\end{equation*} where $\mathcal{B}=\{S\subseteq U: |S|=k\}$.

At first, we suppose that $\ell>0$.  Moreover, from the definition of maximum gain, we can show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-row-average}
\begin{aligned}
   G_S(\tau)&=\sum_{i\in S}\max_{j:\,S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}}
             \nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&=\sum_{i\in Y}\max_{j:\,S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}}
             \nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i}) +\sum_{i\in Z}\max_{j:\,S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}}
             \nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&\ge L_{S}(\tau)\triangleq
 \sum_{y\in Y}\nabla_yF(\tau\1_{S-y})
 +\frac1\ell\sum_{z\in Z}\sum_{o\in X}
       \nabla_oF(\tau\1_{S-z}),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation} where the final inequality comes from that $S=S-y+y\in\mathcal{B}$ when $y\in Y$ and $S-z+x\in\mathcal{B}$ when $z\in Z$ and $o\in X$. 

Secondly, for every $A\subsetneq S$,  we define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-weights}
 w_A=\frac{|Z\setminus A|}{\ell}
      \tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|},
 \qquad w_S=0.
\end{equation}
Then, from the definition of  multilinear extension, we can show that, for any $o\in X$, the following identity holds:
\begin{equation*}
    \frac1\ell\sum_{z\in Z}
       \nabla_oF(\tau\1_{S-z})=\sum_{A\subseteq S} w_A\cdot f(o\mid A)
\end{equation*} Similarly, we also can show that
\begin{equation*}
    \sum_{y\in Y}\nabla_yF(\tau\1_{S-y})=\sum_{A\subseteq S}\sum_{Y\setminus A}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\right)f(y\mid A).
\end{equation*}
As a result, we have that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-1}
    \begin{aligned}
L_S(\tau)&= \sum_{y\in Y}\nabla_yF(\tau\1_{S-y})
 +\frac1\ell\sum_{z\in Z}\sum_{o\in X}
       \nabla_oF(\tau\1_{S-z})\\
&=\sum_{A\subseteq S}\sum_{Y\setminus A}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\right)f(y\mid A)+\sum_{A\subseteq S} w_A\sum_{o\in X}f(o\mid A)\\
 &\ge\sum_{A\subseteq S}w_A
       \sum_{o\in O\setminus A}f(o\mid A)\ge\gamma\sum_{A\subseteq S}w_A
       \bigl(f(A\cup O)-f(A)\bigr)\\&\ge\gamma\left(f(O)-\sum_{A\subseteq S}w_Af(A)\right),
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation} where the first inequality comes from that $\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\ge w_A$ and the second inequality follows from weak submodularity. Moreover, we also can show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-weighted-value}
 \sum_{A\subseteq S}w_Af(A)
 =\frac1\ell\sum_{z\in Z}F(\tau\1_{S-z})
 \le F(\tau\1_S).
\end{equation}
Substitution this Eq.\eqref{eq:cardinality-weighted-value} into
Eq.\eqref{eq:cardinality-1} yields the following result, when $\ell>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-statewise-drift}
 G_S(\tau)\ge\gamma\bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr).
\end{equation}

If $\ell=0$, then $S=O$.  For $R\sim t\1_S$, weak submodularity applied
from $R$ to $S$ gives
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
  &G_S(\tau)=\sum_{i\in S}\max_{j:\,S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}}
             \nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})\ge \sum_{i\in S}
             \nabla_iF(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&=\sum_{A\subsetneq S}\sum_{S\setminus A}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\right)f(y\mid A)=\sum_{A\subsetneq S}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\right)\sum_{S\setminus A}f(y\mid A)\\
             &\ge \sum_{A\subsetneq S}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}\right)(f(S)-f(A))=\frac{\gamma}{1-\tau}\sum_{A\subseteq S}\left(\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-|A|}\right)(f(S)-f(A))\\&\ge\gamma(f(S)-F(t\1_{S}))=\gamma(f(O)-F(t\1_{S})).  
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Thus, according to Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname

    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-lem:conditioned-generator\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:conditioned-generator} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, we now can infer that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cardinality-Q-ode}
 Q'_+(\tau)\ge\gamma\bigl(f(O)-Q(\tau)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Solving Eq.\eqref{eq:cardinality-Q-ode} via Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:potential-comparison\endcsname

      \ref{lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else

    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:potential-comparison}

    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:potential-comparison} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi and using
$Q(\varepsilon)\ge0$ proves Eq.\eqref{eq:cardinality-finite-start}.

<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:factor-lp-semantics}Every feasible point of \eqref{eq:factor-lp} defines a normalized
monotone $\gamma$-weakly submodular function for which the all-good base is
optimal.  If we start at the all-bad base, our proposed \textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ remains at the all-bad base,
so $R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)\le v_{k,0,0}$, where $R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)$ denotes the worst-case approximation ratio of
\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ for matroid-constrained $\gamma$-weakly submodular maximization problems.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Only the trapping claim needs detailed explanation.  After deleting $b_i$, we can show that
\begin{equation*}
\nabla_{b_i}F(\tau\1_{B-b_i})-\nabla_{g_i}F(\tau\1_{B-b_i})=\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{m}\tau^m(1-\tau)^{k-1-m}
 \bigl(v_{m+1,0,0}-v_{m,1,0}\bigr)\ge0,
\end{equation*} where the final inequality comes from the last line of Eq.\eqref{eq:factor-lp}.  Consequently, when initialized at the all-bad base, \textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}\ remains trapped there. As a result, $R_{\textnormal{\texttt{MGPE}}}(\gamma)\le\frac{v_{k,0,0}}{v_{0,k,0}}=v_{k,0,0}$.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:two-sided-repair}Let $\zeta\coloneqq\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$ and $C_\zeta(\tau)\coloneqq
\zeta+(1-\zeta)\tau$. When $f$ is monotone and $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly
submodular, for any feasible exchange $(i,j)$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-sided-repair}
\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)
\geq
\frac{\zeta}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(j\mid R)\bigr]
-
\frac{(1-\zeta)\tau}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $R$ is the random subset drawn from the point
$\tau\1_S$.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
We first consider the case $j=i$. According to the definition of multilinear extension, we can show
\begin{align}
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(i\mid R)\bigr]=
(1-\tau)
\mathbb{E}_{R_0\sim\tau\1_{S-i}}
\bigl[f(i\mid R_0)\bigr]=
(1-\tau)
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\label{eq:two-sided-self-marginal}
\end{align}
Therefore, we can infer that
\begin{align*}
&\frac{\zeta}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(i\mid R)\bigr]
-
\frac{(1-\zeta)\tau}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\leq
\frac{\zeta}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(i\mid R)\bigr]=
\frac{\zeta(1-\tau)}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\leq
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr),
\end{align*}
where the final inequality follows from $\zeta(1-\tau)
\leq
\zeta+(1-\zeta)\tau
=
C_\zeta(\tau)$. Thus, Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-repair} holds when $j=i$. We next consider the case $j\notin S$. Draw $R_0\sim\tau\1_{S-i}$. According to the definition of multilinear extension, we can show
\begin{align}
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(j\mid R)\bigr]
&=
(1-\tau)
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0)\bigr]
+
\tau
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0+i)\bigr].
\label{eq:two-sided-marginal-decomposition}
\end{align}

We now derive a pointwise comparison between
$f(j\mid R_0)$ and $f(j\mid R_0+i)$. Fix a realization of $R_0$
and define $a\coloneqq f(i\mid R_0),
\qquad
b\coloneqq f(j\mid R_0)$, and set 
\[
c\coloneqq
f(R_0+i+j)-f(R_0+i)-f(R_0+j)+f(R_0).
\]
These definitions imply
\begin{align}
f(R_0+i+j)-f(R_0)
&=a+b+c,                                      \label{eq:two-sided-gap}\\
f(j\mid R_0+i)
&=b+c,                                        \label{eq:two-sided-j-after-i}\\
f(i\mid R_0+j)
&=a+c.                                        \label{eq:two-sided-i-after-j}
\end{align}

Applying lower weak submodularity with
$A=R_0$ and $B=R_0+i+j$ yields
\begin{align}
f(i\mid R_0)+f(j\mid R_0)
&\geq
\gamma\bigl(f(R_0+i+j)-f(R_0)\bigr),
\end{align}
or equivalently, $a+b\geq\gamma(a+b+c).$

We next apply upper weak submodularity with the same sets
$A=R_0$ and $B=R_0+i+j$. This gives $f(i\mid R_0+j)+f(j\mid R_0+i)
\leq\beta\bigl(f(R_0+i+j)-f(R_0)\bigr)$. Using Eqs.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-gap}--\eqref{eq:two-sided-i-after-j},
we obtain
\[
(a+c)+(b+c)
\leq
\beta(a+b+c),
\]
and hence $a+b\geq(2-\beta)(a+b+c)$.

Combining the aformentioned two results, we get
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-sided-zeta-pair}
a+b\geq\zeta(a+b+c),
\end{equation} where $\zeta\coloneqq\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}$.
Rearranging Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-zeta-pair}, we have $\zeta c\leq(1-\zeta)(a+b)$ such that
\begin{align*}
f(j\mid R_0+i)=b+c\leq
b+\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta}(a+b)=
\frac{1}{\zeta}b
+
\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta}a.
\end{align*}
Equivalently, $f(j\mid R_0)
\geq
\zeta f(j\mid R_0+i)
-
(1-\zeta)f(i\mid R_0)$.

Taking expectations over
$R_0\sim\tau\1_{S-i}$ yields
\begin{align}
\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)
&=
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0)\bigr]\notag\\
&\geq
\zeta
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0+i)\bigr]
-
(1-\zeta)
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(i\mid R_0)\bigr]\notag\\
&=
\zeta
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0+i)\bigr]
-
(1-\zeta)
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\label{eq:two-sided-expected-repair}
\end{align}

Multiplying Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-expected-repair} by $\tau$
gives
\begin{align}
\tau\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\geq
\zeta\tau
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0+i)\bigr]-
(1-\zeta)\tau
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\label{eq:two-sided-expected-repair-scaled}
\end{align}
From Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-marginal-decomposition}, we have
\begin{align*}
\tau
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0+i)\bigr]
&=
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(j\mid R)\bigr]-
(1-\tau)
\mathbb{E}_{R_0}\bigl[f(j\mid R_0)\bigr]\\
&=
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(j\mid R)\bigr]
-
(1-\tau)
\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\end{align*}
Substituting this identity into
Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-sided-expected-repair-scaled}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\tau\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\geq
\zeta
\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\bigl[f(j\mid R)\bigr]-
\zeta(1-\tau)
\nabla_jF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)-
(1-\zeta)\tau
\nabla_iF\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr).
\end{align*}So we prove Eq.\eqref{eq:two-sided-repair}
<!-- end proof-9 -->

\begin{align}
  \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}
 =\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^*(S,\tau)}F(\tau\1_{S-i}),
\end{align} where $j_i^*(S,\tau)\in\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{j\in\mathcal C_S(i)}
\nabla_jF(\tau\1_{S-i})$. Fix a Brualdi map $h:S\to O$ and draw $R\sim t\1_S$.  Moreover, we order $O\setminus R=\{o_1,\ldots,o_m\}$ and let $i_\ell=h^{-1}(o_\ell)$. Then, for every $\ell\in[m]$, $R\setminus\{i_\ell\}\subseteq R\cup\{o_1,\ldots,o_{\ell-1}\}$. Therefore, weak DR-submodularity gives
\begin{align}
& \sum_{i\in S}f(h(i)\mid R-i)
 \ge\sum_{\ell=1}^m f(o_\ell\mid R-i_\ell)\notag\\
 &\ge\alpha\sum_{\ell=1}^m
 f(o_\ell\mid R+o_1+\cdots+o_{\ell-1})=\alpha\bigl(f(R\cup O)-f(R)\bigr).
 \label{eq:alpha-telescope}
\end{align}
After that, by taking the expectation of $R$ and using monotonicity $f(R\cup O)\ge f(O)$,  we get
\begin{align*}
  \left.\frac{\partial^+V_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{j_i^*}F(\tau\1_{S-i})
 \ge\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_{h(i)}F(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&=\mathbb{E}_{R\sim \tau\1_S}\left[\sum_{i\in S}f(h(i)\mid R-i)\right]\ge\alpha\bigl(f(O)-F(\tau\1_S)\bigr).
\end{align*}
Thus, we get Eq.\eqref{eq:gamma-drift1++} and Eq.\eqref{eq:gamma-drift2++}. 

Next, we prove the results for $(\gamma,\beta)$-weakly submodular objectives. At first, we set $\zeta=\max\{\gamma,2-\beta\}\in(0,1]$. After that, we fix a Brualdi map $h:S\to O$. Then, from Lemma~

At first, from the definition of matroid polytope $P(\mathcal{M})$, we know that, for any $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$, there are coefficients $\lambda_B\ge0$ with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:base-decomposition}
 \sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_B\le 1,
 \qquad \boldsymbol{x}=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_B\1_B.
\end{equation}Then, for any base $S\in\mathcal{B}$, choose a Brualdi bijection $h_{S,B}:S\to B$ and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:balanced-mixture-construction}
 Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\triangleq\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_B\1\{h_{S,B}(i)=j\}.
\end{equation}After that, we verify the matrix $ Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)$ in Eq.\eqref{eq:balanced-mixture-construction} satisfies the three conditions in Definition~ 
    \ifcsname r@def:balanced\endcsname

    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-def:balanced\endcsname

    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-def:balanced} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi. At first, if the element $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)$ is positive, then there exists a base $B$ such that  $j=h_{S,B}(i)$, so $S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}$; For a fixed row $i$, every bijection chooses one image, so $\sum_jQ_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_B\le 1$.  For a fixed column $j$, bijectivity implies that exactly one $i\in S$ maps to $j$ when $j\in B$.  Therefore $\sum_iQ_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)=\sum_B\lambda_B\1\{j\in B\}=x_j$,
where the last equality is coordinate $j$ of the definition of $\boldsymbol{x}$ in Eq.\eqref{eq:base-decomposition}.  Thus, we prove the existence of fractional exchange matrix.

In the remainder of this subsection, for any fixed $\boldsymbol{x}\in P(\mathcal{M})$ and $S\in\mathcal{B}$, we show how to compute its induced fractional exchange matrix $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)$. 

\begin{figure}[t]
    \centering
    \includegraphics[width=0.75\linewidth]
    {fractional_exchange_flow.pdf}
    \caption{The directed network used to construct the fractional
    exchange matrix $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}$.}
    \label{fig:fractional-exchange-flow}
\end{figure}

We first construct a directed network with source $s$ and sink $z$.
Between them, we introduce two layers of vertices: a left vertex for
each $i\in S$ and a right vertex for each $j\in U$. Them, for every
$i\in S$, we add an arc $s\to i$ with capacity at most one. For every pair
$(i,j)\in S\times U$ satisfying $S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}$, we add an arc
$i\to j$ with unbounded capacity. Finally, for every $j\in U$, we add
an arc $j\to z$ with capacity $x_j$, as illustrated in
Figure~ 
    \ifcsname r@fig:fractional-exchange-flow\endcsname

      \ref{main-main-fig:fractional-exchange-flow} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-fig:fractional-exchange-flow} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-fig:fractional-exchange-flow\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-fig:fractional-exchange-flow} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-fig:fractional-exchange-flow} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi. We then seek a feasible
$s$-$z$ flow of value $\sum_{j\in U}x_j$, where $\boldsymbol{x}\triangleq(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$. Given such a feasible flow
$\varphi$, we define
\[
Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\triangleq
\begin{cases}
\varphi(i,j),
    & \text{if } S-i+j\in\mathcal{B},\\
0,
    & \text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
The source capacities ensure that the row sums of $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}$ are at most
one, the capacity of the arcs entering $z$ gives the prescribed
column sums $x_j$, and the construction of the exchange arcs guarantees
feasible support. Hence, $Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}$ is a fractional exchange matrix.

Because the resulting network contains $k+n$ non-terminal vertices and at most
$kn$ exchange arcs, according to the maximum-flow algorithm of
\citet{orlin2013maxflows},  a feasible flow can therefore be computed in $O\bigl(kn(k+n)\bigr)$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
We first prove point (i). If there exist some $i\in S$ making $\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)<1$, we then can show $\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)<1$. But, from the definition of Insertion marginals, we have $\sum_{i\in S}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)=x_j$ such that $\sum_{j\in U}\sum_{i\in S}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)=\sum_{j\in U}x_j=k$. By contradiction, we have $\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)=1$ for every $i\in $.

As for point (ii), for any $j\notin S$, we have $\sum_{i\in S}Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)=0$ such that $Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)=0$ for any $j\notin S$ and $i\in S$. Then, for any $j\in S$, from the feasible support condition, we know that, only when $i=j$, $S-i+j\in\mathcal{B}$ such that $Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)=0$ for any $j\in S\setminus\{i\}$. Moreover, according to the previous point (i), when $j\in S$, we have $Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)=\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)=1$. Finally, we have when $\boldsymbol{x}=\1_S$, then  $Q_S^{\1_S}(i,j)=\1\{i=j\}$ for every $i\in S$ and $j\in U$.
<!-- end proof-12 -->

    \fi, we suppose the rate $ \lambda(\tau)=\frac{k}{\tau}$ and we select exchange $(i,j)\sim\frac{Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)}{k}$ such that 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
      \left.\frac{\partial^+V^{t}_{S,\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_S)
  +\tau\lambda(\tau)\mathbb{E}_{(i,j)\sim p(\tau,S)}
  \left[\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})-\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})\right]\\
  &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_S)
  +k\cdot \mathbb{E}_{(i,j)\sim \frac{Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}}{k}}
  \left[\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})-\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})\right]\\
  &=\sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_S)
  +\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)
  \left(\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})-\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})\right)\\
  &=\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i}),
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the final identity comes from $\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}}_{S}(i,j)=1$.

&\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}

\end{equation}where $C_\gamma(\tau)=\gamma+(1-\gamma)\tau$. Furthermore, from the point \textbf{(ii)} in Theorem~

\begin{equation*}
   \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})=\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-j}).
\end{equation*}As a result, if we set $a_{\gamma}(\tau)=\frac{\gamma+(1-\gamma)\tau}
        {\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}$, we can infer that
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\gamma}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)+(1-a_{\gamma}(\tau))Q_S^{\1_{S}}(i,j)\right)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\sum_{j\in U}x_i\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]       
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}From the definition of multilinear extension, we know that the $j$-th coordinate of $\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}$ satisfies $g_{t,S,\tau}(j)=\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]$. So we have 
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\gamma}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)+(1-a_{\gamma}(\tau))Q_S^{\1_{S}}(i,j)\right)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\sum_{j\in U}x_i\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]=\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
       &=\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
      &=\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\sum_{j\in O}\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
       &\ge\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S}))+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle,
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}where the final inequality comes from the $\gamma$-weak submodularity.

    \fi. Before going into the details, we define some useful notation. Firstly, we rewrite the evolution of fractional point $\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE} algorithm as $\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau),\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$. Specifically, for any $\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$, we set $r^{max}(\tau)\triangleq\max_{0\le r\le N}\{r:\tau_r\le\tau\}$ with $\tau_0=\epsilon$. Then, we can infer that 
\begin{equation*}
\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)\triangleq\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}Similarly, we also reset the the evolution of fractional point $S_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE} algorithm as 
\begin{equation*}
    S_t(\tau)\triangleq S_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}

    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} Here, we also set the evolution of $a_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE} algorithm as $ a_t(\tau)\triangleq a_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$.

    \ifcsname r@main-add_important\endcsname
      \ref{main-add_important} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-add_important} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-add_important\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-add_important} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-add_important} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-add_important\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-add_important\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-add_important} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-add_important} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-add_important\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-add_important} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-add_important} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, we get 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
         \left.\frac{\partial^+V^{t}_{S_t(\tau),\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&=\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{t}(\tau)Q^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)+(1-a_{t}(\tau)Q^{\1_{S_t(\tau)}}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j))\right)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i})\\
&=\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\gamma}(\tau)Q^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)+(1-a_{\gamma}(\tau)Q^{\1_{S_t(\tau)}}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j))\right)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i})\\
&+\left(a_t(\tau)-a_{\gamma}(\tau)\right)\cdot\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\left(Q^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)-Q^{\1_{S_t(\tau)}}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j))\right)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i})
         \\&\ge\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)}))+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau}\rangle\\&+\left(a_t(\tau)-a_{\gamma}(\tau)\right)\cdot\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\left(Q^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)-Q^{\1_{S_t(\tau)}}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j))\right)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i}).
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}For notational convenience, we also define \begin{equation*}
    \Delta_{t,r}\triangleq\sum_{i\in S_{t,r-1}}\sum_{j\in U}
\left(
    Q_{S_{t,r-1}}^{\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r}}(i,j)
    -
    Q_{S_{t,r-1}}^{\1_{S_{t,r-1}}}(i,j)
\right)
\nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau_r\1_{S_{t,r-1}-i}\bigr).
\end{equation*} and set $\Delta_t(\tau)\triangleq\Delta_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$.

As a result, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{add_sum}
    \begin{aligned}
\left.\frac{\partial^+\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}V^{t}_{S_t(\tau),\tau}(r)\right)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&\ge \frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)})\right)\\&+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau}\rangle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(a_t(\tau)-a_{\gamma}(\tau)\right)\Delta_t(\tau).
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}

If we set $Q^{(T)}(\tau)\triangleq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)})]$, from Eq.\eqref{add_sum}, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
        \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&\ge \frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)\\&+\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau}\rangle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(a_t(\tau)-a_{\gamma}(\tau)\right)\Delta_t(\tau).
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}

Furthermore, under Assumption~

\begin{equation}\label{add_regret}
\begin{aligned}
      \mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\1_{O}-\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau),\mathbf{g}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau}\rangle\right]&\le O\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\right),\\
      \mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(a_t(\tau)-a_{\gamma}(\tau)\right)\Delta_t(\tau)\right]&\le O\left(k\sqrt{T}\right).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

By applying the previous regret results Eq.\eqref{add_regret} about oracle $\mathcal{E}$ and weight oracle $\mathcal{A}$, we immediately obtain:
\begin{equation*}
    \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\ge \frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)-M_{0}\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}+k\sqrt{T}\right),
\end{equation*} where we assume $M_0$ is the hidden large constant in Eq.\eqref{add_regret}.

\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
       Q^{(T)}(1)&\ge e^{-H(1)}Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)+(1-e^{-H(1)}Q^{(T)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O) \\
       &-M_{0}\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}+k\sqrt{T}\right)\int_{\epsilon}^{1}\exp{\left(-\left(H(1)-H(s)\right)\right)}\mathrm{d}s,
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where $H(\tau)\triangleq\int_{\epsilon}^{\tau}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)u}\mathrm{d}u=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2(1-\gamma)}\log(\frac{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\epsilon})$. Note that $H(\tau)=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2(1-\gamma)}\log(\frac{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\tau}{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\epsilon})$ is a non-decreasing function about the variable $\tau$ such that  $\exp{\left(-\left(H(1)-H(s)\right)\right)}\le 1$.

As a result, we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
        Q^{(T)}(1)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}(S_t)]&\ge (1-e^{-H(1)}Q^{(T)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}+k\sqrt{T}\right)\\
        &=\left(1-(\frac{\gamma+2(1-\gamma)\epsilon}{2-\gamma})^{\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2(1-\gamma)}}\right)\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}+k\sqrt{T}\right)\\&\ge(1-\rho_{\gamma}-\epsilon)\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\left(k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}+k\sqrt{T}\right),
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the first inequality comes from $Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)\ge0$ and the final inequality comes from Lemma~

    \begin{aligned}
&\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}
    Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\zeta}{C_\zeta(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\sum_{i\in S}Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]-\frac{(1-\zeta)\tau}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
       \sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i}),\\
&=\frac{\zeta}{C_\zeta(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}x_i\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]-\frac{(1-\zeta)\tau}{C_\zeta(\tau)}
       \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i}),
    \end{aligned} 
\end{equation}where $C_\zeta(\tau)=\zeta+(1-\zeta)\tau$. Furthermore, from the point \textbf{(ii)} in Theorem~

    \ifcsname r@main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties\endcsname
      \ref{main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-thm:fractional-exchange-properties} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, we can rewrite 
\begin{equation*}
   \sum_{i\in S}\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})=\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\1_{S}}_{S}(i,j)\nabla_iF_t(\tau\1_{S-j}).
\end{equation*}As a result, if we set $a_{\zeta}(\tau)=\frac{\zeta+(1-\zeta)\tau}
        {\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}$, we can infer that
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\zeta}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)+(1-a_{\zeta}(\tau))Q_S^{\1_{S}}(i,j)\right)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\sum_{j\in U}x_i\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]       
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}From the definition of multilinear extension, we know that the $j$-th coordinate of $\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}$ satisfies $g_{t,S,\tau}(j)=\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]$. So we have 
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}\left(a_{\zeta}(\tau)Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)+(1-a_{\zeta}(\tau))Q_S^{\1_{S}}(i,j)\right)
    \nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&\ge\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\sum_{j\in U}x_i\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]=\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
       &=\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\langle\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle+\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
      &=\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\sum_{j\in O}\cdot
       \mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_{S}}[f_t(j\mid R)]+\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\textbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle\\
       &\ge\frac{\zeta\gamma}{\gamma+2(1-\zeta)\tau}(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S}))+\frac{\zeta}{\zeta+2(1-\zeta)\tau}\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\mathbf{g}_{t,S,\tau}\rangle,
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}where the final inequality comes from the $\gamma$-weak submodularity.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
We write $X=O\setminus S$ and $Y=O\cap S$. Then, we can show that 
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{j\in U}\sum_{i\in S}\bar Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\&=\sum_{j\in S}\sum_{i\in S}\bar Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)+\sum_{j\in U\setminus S}\sum_{i\in S}\bar Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{j\in S}x_j\nabla_j F_t(\tau\1_{S-j})
 +\sum_{j\in U\setminus S}\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)x_j
   \nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&=\langle\boldsymbol{x},\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle=\langle\1_{O},\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{x}-\1_O,\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}

Then, from the definition of $\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}$, we can show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:card-online-comparator-expand}
    \begin{aligned}
\langle\1_{O},\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle   =\sum_{z\in Y}\nabla_z F_t(\tau\1_{S-z})
 +\sum_{o\in X }\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)
   \nabla_oF_t(\tau\1_{S-i})
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}


For $A\subsetneq S$, we set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:card-online-weights}
 b_A=\tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|},
 \qquad
 w_A=b_A\sum_{i\in S\setminus A}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S),
 \qquad w_S=0.
\end{equation}
According to the multilinear expansion of
Eq.\eqref{eq:card-online-comparator-expand}, we can know that the coefficient of
$f_t(o\mid A)$ is $w_A$ for every $o\in X$.  For every
$z\in Y\setminus A$, the coefficient of $f_t(z\mid A)$ is $b_A$. Thus, we can show that        
                                                                                           
\begin{align}\left\langle\1_O,\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,y}\right\rangle
 &\ge\sum_{A\subseteq S}\sum_{z\in Y\setminus A}b_A\cdot f_t(z\mid A)+\sum_{A\subseteq S}\sum_{o\in X}w_A\cdot f_t(o\mid A)\\&\ge\sum_{A\subseteq S}w_A\sum_{o\in O\setminus A}f_t(o\mid A)\ge\gamma\sum_{A\subseteq S}w_A
       \bigl(f_t(O)-f_t(A)\bigr),
 \label{eq:card-online-weak-average}
\end{align}
where the second inequality comes from  and
$b_A\ge w_A$ due to that $\sum_{i\in S\setminus A}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)\le1$. The weights form a probability distribution:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:card-online-weight-sum}
 \sum_{A\subseteq S}w_A
 =\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)\sum_{A\subseteq S-i}
   \tau^{|A|}(1-\tau)^{k-1-|A|}
 =\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)=1.
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:card-online-weighted-value}
 \sum_{A\subseteq S}w_Af_t(A)
 =\sum_{i\in S}p_i(\boldsymbol{x},S)F_t(\tau\1_{S-i})
 \le F_t(\tau\1_S).
\end{equation}
Substituting
Eq.\eqref{eq:card-online-weight-sum} and Eq.\eqref{eq:card-online-weighted-value}
into Eq.\eqref{eq:card-online-weak-average} proves
\begin{equation*}
 \left\langle\1_O,\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S,\tau,y}\right\rangle\ge\gamma(f_{t}(O)-F_t(\tau\1_S)).   
\end{equation*} Thus, we get the result of Eq.\eqref{eq:online-card-drift}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->

      \ref{main-main-main-main-thm:ope-card-regret} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi. Before going into the details, we define some useful notation. Firstly, we rewrite the evolution of fractional point $\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPEC} algorithm as $\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau),\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$. Specifically, for any $\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$, we set $r^{max}(\tau)\triangleq\max_{0\le r\le N}\{r:\tau_r\le\tau\}$ with $\tau_0=\epsilon$. Then, we can infer that 
\begin{equation*}
\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)\triangleq\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}Similarly, we also reset the the evolution of fractional point $S_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE} algorithm as 
\begin{equation*}
    S_t(\tau)\triangleq S_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}
Then, according to Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:32\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:32\endcsname

    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:32} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi and Line 8 in \texttt{OPEC} algorithm, we can know that
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
      \left.\frac{\partial^+V^{t}_{S_t(\tau),\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&=\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}\bar{Q}^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i})\\
      &\ge\gamma(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)}))+\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau,\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}\rangle.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} 

If we set $Q^{(T)}(\tau)\triangleq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)})]$,  we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
        \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\ge \gamma\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau,\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}\rangle
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}

Furthermore, under Assumption~ 
    \ifcsname r@ass:ope-oracles2\endcsname

    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, we can have 
\begin{equation}\label{add_regret2}
\begin{aligned}
      \mathbb{E}\left[
            \sum_{t=1}^T
            \langle\1_{O}-\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau),\ell^{\mathrm{card}}_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau,\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}\rangle
        \right]
        \leq \mathcal{O}\left(
            k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\right).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

By applying the previous regret results Eq.\eqref{add_regret2} about oracle $\mathcal{E}$, we immediately obtain:
\begin{equation*}
    \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\ge \gamma\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)-M_{0}k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T},
\end{equation*} where we assume $M_0$ is the hidden large constant in Eq.\eqref{add_regret2}.

    \fi, we can show that
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
       Q^{(T)}(1)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}(S_t)]&\ge e^{-\gamma(1-\epsilon)}Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)+(1-e^{-\gamma(1-\epsilon)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O) \\
     &-M_{0}\cdot k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\cdot\int_{\epsilon}^{1}\exp{\left(-\gamma(1-s)\right)}\mathrm{d}s\\
     &\ge (1-e^{-\gamma(1-\epsilon)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\cdot k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\\
     &\ge(1-e^{-\gamma}-\epsilon)\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\cdot k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T} ,
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the second inequality comes from $Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)\ge0$ and $\exp{\left(-\gamma(1-s)\right)}\le 1$ when $s\in[\epsilon,1]$, and the final inequality follows from $e^{-\gamma(1-\epsilon)})\le e^{-\gamma}-\epsilon$. So we can get Eq.\eqref{eq:ope-card-regret} of Theorem~ 
    \ifcsname r@thm:ope-card-regret\endcsname

<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
From the definition of Eq.\eqref{equ:ell}, we can show that
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
      &\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in U}
Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)
\nabla_j F_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)=\sum_{j\in U}y_j\sum_{i\in S}
\frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,j)}{x_j}
\nabla_j F_t\bigl(\tau\1_{S-i}\bigr)\\
&=\langle\boldsymbol{x},\ell_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle=\langle\1_{O},\ell_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle+\langle
\boldsymbol{x}-\1_{O},\ell_{t,S,\tau,\mathbf{y}}\rangle.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}  According to the definition of multilinear extension, we have 
\begin{align*}
 \langle\1_{O},\ell_{t,S,\tau,\boldsymbol{x}}\rangle& =\sum_{o\in O}\sum_{i\in S}
 \frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,o)}{x_o}\nabla_{o} F_t(\tau\1_{S-i})\\&\ge\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\left[\sum_{o\in O\setminus R}\sum_{i\in S}
 \frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,o)}{x_o}f_t(o\mid R-i)\right]\\&\ge\alpha\cdot\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\left[\sum_{o\in O\setminus R}\sum_{i\in S}
 \frac{Q_S^{\boldsymbol{x}}(i,o)}{x_o}f_t(o_q\mid R+o_1+\cdots+o_{q-1})\right]\\&=\alpha\cdot\mathbb{E}_{R\sim\tau\1_S}\left[\sum_{o\in O\setminus R}f_t(o_q\mid R+o_1+\cdots+o_{q-1})\right]\\&\ge\alpha(f_t(O)-F_t(\tau\1_S)),
\end{align*} where the second inequality uses $R-i\subseteq R+o_1+\cdots+o_{q-1}$ and weak DR-submodularity.
<!-- end proof-19 -->

    \fi. Before going into the details, we define some useful notation. Firstly, we rewrite the evolution of fractional points $\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r},\mathbf{y}_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE-DR} algorithm as $\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau),\mathbf{y}_{t}(\tau),\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$. Specifically, for any $\tau\in[\epsilon,1]$, we set $r^{max}(\tau)\triangleq\max_{0\le r\le N}\{r:\tau_r\le\tau\}$ with $\tau_0=\epsilon$. Then, we can infer that 
\begin{equation*}
\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)\triangleq\boldsymbol{x}_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\quad\mathbf{y}_t(\tau)\triangleq\mathbf{y}_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\quad\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}Similarly, we also reset the the evolution of fractional point $S_{t,r},\forall r\in N$ in \texttt{OPE} algorithm as 
\begin{equation*}
    S_t(\tau)\triangleq S_{t,r^{max}(\tau)},\forall\tau\in[\epsilon,1].
\end{equation*}
Then, according to Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:online-alpha-drift\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:online-alpha-drift\endcsname

\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
      \left.\frac{\partial^+V^{t}_{S_t(\tau),\tau}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}&=\sum_{i\in S_t(\tau)}\sum_{j\in U}Q^{\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}_{S_t(\tau)}(i,j)\nabla_jF_t(\tau\1_{S_t(\tau)-i})\\
      &\ge\alpha(f_t(O)-F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)}))+\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\ell_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau,\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}\rangle.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} 

If we set $Q^{(T)}(\tau)\triangleq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[F_{t}(\tau 1_{S_t(\tau)})]$,  we have 
\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
        \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\ge \alpha\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)+(1-\epsilon)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)-\1_{O},\ell_{t,S_t(\tau),\tau,\boldsymbol{x}_t(\tau)}\rangle
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}

\begin{equation}\label{add_regret1}
\begin{aligned}
      \mathbb{E}\left[

        \right]
        \leq \mathcal{O}\left(
            k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\right).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

By applying the previous regret results Eq.\eqref{add_regret1} about oracle $\mathcal{E}$, we immediately obtain:
\begin{equation*}
    \left.\frac{\partial^+Q^{(T)}(r)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=\tau}\ge \alpha\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-Q^{(T)}(\tau)\right)-(1-\epsilon)M_{0}k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T},
\end{equation*} where we assume $M_0$ is the hidden large constant in Eq.\eqref{add_regret1}.

\begin{equation*}
    \begin{aligned}
       Q^{(T)}(1)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[f_{t}(S_t)]&\ge e^{-\alpha(1-\epsilon)}Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)+(1-e^{-\alpha(1-\epsilon)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O) \\
     &-M_{0}k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\cdot \int_{\epsilon}^{1}\exp{\left(-\alpha(1-s)\right)}\mathrm{d}s\\
     &\ge (1-e^{-\alpha(1-\epsilon)})\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\cdot k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T}\\
     &\ge(1-e^{-\alpha}-\epsilon)\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_t(O)-M_{0}\cdot k\sqrt{\log\left(n/k\right)T},
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation*} where the second inequality comes from $Q^{(T)}(\epsilon)\ge0$ and $\exp{\left(-\alpha(1-s)\right)}\le 1$ when $s\in[\epsilon,1]$, and the final inequality comes from  $e^{-\alpha(1-\epsilon)}\le e^{-\alpha}-\epsilon$. So we can get Eq.\eqref{eq:ope-alpha-regret} of Theorem~
