<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:E-fractional-augmentation}
Let $\mathbf a,\mathbf b\in P(\mathcal{M})$ satisfy
$a(U)<b(U)$. Then there exist an element $j\in U$ and a scalar
$\delta>0$ such that
\[
a_j<b_j
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\mathbf a+\delta\mathbf e_j\in P(\mathcal{M}).
\]
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $J\coloneqq\{j\in U:a_j<b_j\}$. We suppose, toward a contradiction, that no coordinate in $J$ can be
increased while preserving feasibility. Then, for every $j\in J$,
there exists a set $A_j\subseteq U$ containing $j$ that is tight at
$\mathbf a$, i.e.,
\[
a(A_j)=r_{\mathcal{M}}(A_j),
\] where $r_{\mathcal{M}}(A)\triangleq\max_{I\in\mathcal{I},I\subseteq A}|I|$ is the rank function of matroid $\mathcal{M}$. Note that the family of tight sets is closed under unions. More specifically, due to the submodularity of rank function $r_{\mathcal{M}}$, for any two tight sets $C$ and $D$, we have
\begin{align*}
&r_{\mathcal{M}}(C\cup D)+r_{\mathcal{M}}(C\cap D)\ge
a(C\cup D)+a(C\cap D)
\\&=a(C)+a(D)=r_{\mathcal{M}}(C)+r_{\mathcal{M}}(D)\\
&\geq r_{\mathcal{M}}(C\cup D)+r_{\mathcal{M}}(C\cap D)\geq a(C\cup D)+a(C\cap D),
\end{align*} where the first equality comes from the linearity of $a$. As a result, if we set $A\coloneqq\bigcup_{j\in J}A_j$, we can show  $a(A)=r_{\mathcal{M}}(A)$. 
For every $j\notin A$, we have $j\notin J$ and hence
$b_j\leq a_j$. Consequently,
\[
b(U)
=
b(A)+b(U\setminus A)
\leq
r_{\mathcal{M}}(A)+a(U\setminus A)=a(A)+a(U\setminus A)
=
a(U),
\]
contradicting $a(U)<b(U)$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:E-entropy-center}
The initialization $\boldsymbol{x}_1$ is strictly positive and full-rank. Moreover, for every $\mathbf u\in P(\mathcal{M})$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:E-entropy-diameter}
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_1)
\leq
k\log\left(\frac{en}{k}\right).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Before going into the details, we fix a base $B^{(u)}\in\mathcal{B}$ containing $u$ for every $u\in U$ and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:E-positive-witness}
\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}
\coloneqq
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{u\in U}\1_{B^{(u)}}.
\end{equation}

We first establish the positivity of $\boldsymbol{x}^{o}$. We suppose, for
contradiction, that $\boldsymbol{x}_1(j)=0$ for some $j\in U$, and set  $Z\coloneqq\{i\in U: \boldsymbol{x}_1(i)=0\}$. Then, for any $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, let
\[
\boldsymbol{x}_\varepsilon
\coloneqq
(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{x}_1
+\varepsilon\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}.
\]
By the convexity of $P(\mathcal{M})$, we have
$\boldsymbol{x}_\varepsilon\in P(\mathcal{M})$. Recall that $\Psi(\boldsymbol{x})
=
\sum_{j\in U}\bigl(x_j\log x_j-x_j\bigr)$, where $0\log 0\coloneqq0$. For every $j\in Z$, we have
$\boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)=\varepsilon\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)$, and hence
\begin{align*}
&\boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)\log \boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)
-\boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)
-\bigl(\boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)\log \boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)-\boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)\bigr)\\
&\qquad
=
\varepsilon\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)\log\varepsilon
+\varepsilon\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)
\bigl(\log \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)-1\bigr).
\end{align*}
On the other hand, for each $j\notin Z$, the function
$u\mapsto u\log u-u$ is continuously differentiable in a neighborhood
of $\boldsymbol{x}_1(j)>0$. Therefore,
\[
\boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)\log \boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)
-\boldsymbol{x}_{\varepsilon}(j)
-\bigl(\boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)\log \boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)-\boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)\bigr)
=
\varepsilon
(\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)-\boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j))\log \boldsymbol{x}_{1}(j)
+O(\varepsilon^2).
\]
Summing over all coordinates gives
\[
\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_\varepsilon)-\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1)
=
\varepsilon
\left(\sum_{j\in Z}\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)\right)\log\varepsilon
+O(\varepsilon).
\]
Since $\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)>0$ for every $j\in U$ and $Z\neq\varnothing$, we have $\sum_{j\in Z}\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)>0$. Consequently,
\[
\frac{\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_\varepsilon)-\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1)}{\varepsilon}
=
\left(\sum_{j\in Z}\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)\right)\log\varepsilon
+O(1)
\longrightarrow-\infty
\qquad\text{as }\varepsilon\downarrow0.
\]
Thus, $\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_\varepsilon)<\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1)$ for all sufficiently small
$\varepsilon>0$, contradicting the optimality of $\boldsymbol{x}_1$. Hence,
$\boldsymbol{x}_1(j)>0$ for every $j\in U$.


Then, we suppose that $\boldsymbol{x}_1(U)<k=\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(U)$. By
Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, there exist $j\in U$ and
$\delta>0$ such that
\[
\boldsymbol{x}_1(j)<\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}(j)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\boldsymbol{x}_1+\delta\mathbf e_j\in P(\mathcal{M}).
\]
Because $\boldsymbol{x}_1(j)<1$, $\frac{\partial\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1)}{\partial x_j}
=
\log(\boldsymbol{x}_1(j))<0$. Therefore, increasing coordinate $j$
slightly strictly will decrease $\Psi$, again contradicting the optimality of $\boldsymbol{x}_1$. Hence $\boldsymbol{x}_1(U)=k$. Finally, the first-order optimality gives $\left\langle
\nabla\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1),
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_1
\right\rangle
\geq0$, and hence
\[
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_1)
\leq
\Psi(\mathbf u)-\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1).
\]
Since every coordinate of $\mathbf u$ lies in $[0,1]$,
$\Psi(\mathbf u)\leq0$. Moreover, we also know that 
\[
\min_{\boldsymbol{x}(U)=k,\boldsymbol{x}\ge0}\Psi(\boldsymbol{x})=\Psi(\frac{k\cdot\1_{U}}{n})= k\log\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)-k.
\]
Since $\boldsymbol{x}_1(U)=k$, it follows that $\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_1)
\geq
k\log\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)-k$. Consequently,
\[
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_1)
\leq
k\log\left(\frac{n}{k}\right)+k
=
k\log\left(\frac{en}{k}\right).
\]
<!-- end proof-22 -->

<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
The claim holds at initialization by
Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-entropy-center\endcsname

      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-entropy-center} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else

      \ref{main-main-lem:E-entropy-center}

    \fi

    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:E-entropy-center} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi. Suppose that $\boldsymbol{x}_t$ is
strictly positive and satisfies $\boldsymbol{x}_t(U)=k$. Since
$\tilde{\mathbf g}_t\geq\mathbf0$, $z_{t+1}(j)
=
x_{t}(j)
\exp\bigl(
    \eta_{\mathcal E}\tilde g_t(j)
\bigr)
\geq
x_{t}(j)>0$.

Suppose, for contradiction, that $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}$ has at least one zero
coordinate, and let $Z\coloneqq\{j\in U:\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)=0\}$. Then, for any $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, define
\[
\mathbf{y}_{\epsilon}
\coloneqq
(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}
+\varepsilon\overline{\boldsymbol{x}},
\]
where $\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}\in P(\mathcal{M})$ is defined in Eq.\eqref{eq:E-positive-witness}. For each coordinate $j\in U$,  we define 
\begin{equation*}
    \phi_j(u)
\coloneqq
u\log\frac{u}{z_{t+1}(j)}-u+z_{t+1}(j),
\end{equation*} so that $D_{\Psi}(\boldsymbol{x},\mathbf z_{t+1})
=
\sum_{j\in U}\phi_j(\boldsymbol{x}(j))$. For every $j\in Z$, we have
$\mathbf{y}_{\epsilon}(j)=\varepsilon\overline{x}(j)$, and therefore
\begin{align*}
\phi_j(\mathbf{y}_{\epsilon}(j))-\phi_j(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j))
&=
\varepsilon\overline{x}(j)
\log\frac{\epsilon\overline{x}(j)}{z_{t+1}(j)}
-\varepsilon\overline{x}(j)\\
&=
\varepsilon\overline{x}(j)\log\varepsilon
+\varepsilon\overline{x}(j)
\left(
\log\frac{\overline{x}(j)}{z_{t+1}(j)}-1
\right).
\end{align*}
For every $j\notin Z$, we have $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)>0$. Since $\phi_j$ is continuously
differentiable in a neighborhood of $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)$, with $\phi_j'(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j))=\log\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)}{z_{t+1}(j)}$,
a first-order expansion gives
\[
\phi_j(\mathbf{y}_{\epsilon}(j))-\phi_j(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j))
=
\varepsilon\cdot(\overline{x}(j)-\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j))
\log\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)}{z_{t+1}(j)}
+O(\varepsilon^2).
\]
Summing over all coordinates, we obtain
\[
D_{\Psi}(\mathbf y_\varepsilon,\mathbf z_{t+1})
-
D_{\Psi}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1})
=
\varepsilon
\left(\sum_{j\in Z}\overline{x}(j)\right)
\log\varepsilon
+O(\varepsilon).
\]
Because $\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}$ is strictly positive and $Z\neq\varnothing$, we have 
\[
\frac{
D_{\Psi}(\mathbf y_\varepsilon,\mathbf z_{t+1})
-
D_{\Psi}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1})
}{\varepsilon}
=
\left(\sum_{j\in Z}\overline{x}(j)\right)\log\varepsilon
+O(1)
\longrightarrow-\infty
\]
as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. Consequently, $D_{\Psi}(\mathbf y_\varepsilon,\mathbf z_{t+1})
<
D_{\Psi}(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1})$ for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$. This contradicts the
optimality of $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}$. Hence, every coordinate of
$\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}$ is strictly positive.


We next claim that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:E-y-below-z}
\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)\leq z_{t+1}(j)
\qquad
\text{for every }j\in U.
\end{equation}
If $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)>z_{t+1}(j)$, we set 
\begin{equation*}
    \mathbf{y}(b)=\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}-b\cdot(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)-z_{t+1}(j))\mathbf{e}_j.
\end{equation*}Due to the down-closedness of $P(\mathcal{M})$, we have $\mathbf{y}(b)\in P(\mathcal{M})$ for any $b\in[0,1]$.Similarly, we can show 
\[
\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}D_\Psi(\mathbf{y}(b),\mathbf z_{t+1})}{\mathrm{d}b}\right|_{b=0}=-(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)-z_{t+1}(j))\log\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)}{z_{t+1}(j)}\le 0.
\] Thus,there exists $b\ge$ such that $D_\Psi(\mathbf{y}(b),\mathbf z_{t+1})\le D_\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1})$.
This contradicts the optimality of $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}$. Suppose now that $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(U)<k=\boldsymbol{x}_t(U)$. By Lemma~ 
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname

      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation}

      \ref{main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-lem:E-fractional-augmentation} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi, there exist $j\in U$ and
$\delta>0$ such that
\[
\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)<\boldsymbol{x}_{t}(j)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}+\delta\mathbf e_j\in P(\mathcal{M}).
\]
According to Line 5 in Algorithm~ 
    \ifcsname r@alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \else
       
    \ifcsname r@main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd\endcsname
      \ref{main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \else
      \ref{main-main-main-main-alg:matroid-entropy-omd} 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi 
    \fi and $\tilde{g}_t(t)\ge0$, we also have $x_{t+1}(j)<\boldsymbol{x}_{t}(j)\leq z_{t+1}(j)$. Hence, for all sufficiently small $\delta'>0$, $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}+\delta'\mathbf e_j\in P(\mathcal{M})$ and $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)+\delta'<z_{t+1}(j)$ such that 

\[
\frac{\mathrm{d}D_\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}+\delta'\mathbf e_j,\mathbf z_{t+1})}{\mathrm{d}\delta'}=\log\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(j)+\delta'}{z_{t+1}(j)}< 0.
\] 
It follows that
\[
D_\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}+\delta'\mathbf e_j,\mathbf z_{t+1})
<
D_\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1}),
\]
again contradicting the optimality of $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}$. Therefore, $\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1}(U)=k$.

<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
The Pythagorean inequality of Bregman divergence  gives
\[
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\mathbf z_{t+1})
\geq
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1})
+
D_\Psi(\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1},\mathbf z_{t+1}).
\]
Since Bregman divergences are nonnegative, it follows that $D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1})
\leq
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\mathbf z_{t+1})$,

By the definition of the
generalized KL divergence, we also have 
\begin{align*}
&D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\mathbf z_{t+1})
-D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_t)\\
&=
\sum_{j\in U}
u_j\log\frac{x_{t,j}}{z_{t+1,j}}
+
\sum_{j\in U}(z_{t+1,j}-x_{t,j})\\
&=-\eta
\left\langle
\mathbf u,\tilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle
+\sum_{j\in U}x_{t,j}
\left(
e^{\eta\widetilde g_t(j)}-1\right),
\end{align*} where the second equality comes from $\log\frac{x_{t,j}}{z_{t+1,j}}
=
-\eta\widetilde g_t(j)$ and $z_{t+1,j}-x_{t,j}
=
x_{t,j}
\left(
e^{\eta\tilde g_t(j)}-1
\right)$.

Substituting these identities yields
\begin{align}
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1})
&\leq D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\mathbf z_{t+1})\notag\\
&=
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_t)
-\eta
\left\langle
\mathbf u,\tilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle
+\sum_{j\in U}x_{t,j}
\left(
e^{\eta\tilde g_t(j)}-1
\right).
\label{eq:kl-one-step-expansion}
\end{align}

Since
$0\leq\eta\tilde g_t(j)\leq1$ and
$e^v\leq1+v+v^2$ for every $v\in[0,1]$, we have
\[
e^{\eta\tilde g_t(j)}-1
\leq
\eta\tilde g_t(j)
+\eta^2\tilde g_t(j)^2.
\]
Applying this inequality to
Eq.~\eqref{eq:kl-one-step-expansion} gives
\begin{align*}
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1})
&\leq
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_t)
-\eta
\left\langle
\mathbf u,\tilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle\\
&\quad
+\eta
\left\langle
\boldsymbol{x}_t,\tilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle
+\eta^2
\sum_{j\in U}
x_{t,j}\tilde g_t(j)^2\\
&=
D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_t)
-\eta
\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\tilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle
+\eta^2
\sum_{j\in U}
x_{t,j}\tilde g_t(j)^2.
\end{align*}
Then, the following inequality holds:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:entropy-one-step}
\begin{aligned}
  \mathbb{E}[\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\mathbf g_t
\right\rangle]&=\mathbb{E}[\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\mathbb{E}_t[\widetilde{\mathbf g}_t]\right\rangle=\mathbb{E}[\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\widetilde{\mathbf g}_t
\right\rangle]
\\&\leq
\frac{
\mathbb{E}[D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_t)]
-
\mathbb{E}[D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_{t+1})]
}{\eta}
+
\eta
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j\in U}
x_{t,j}\tilde g_t(j)^2].  
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

Summing Eq.~\eqref{eq:entropy-one-step}, using
$\boldsymbol{x}_t(U)=k$, and applying $D_\Psi(\mathbf u,\boldsymbol{x}_1)
\leq k\frac{en}{k}$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:entropy-pathwise-regret}
\sum_{t=1}^T
\mathbb{E}[\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\mathbf g_t
\right\rangle]
\leq
\frac{k\log(\frac{en}{k})}{\eta}
+\eta G^{2}kT.
\end{equation}
As a result, we have  $\sum_{t=1}^T
\mathbb{E}[\left\langle
\mathbf u-\boldsymbol{x}_t,\mathbf g_t
\right\rangle]\leq kG\sqrt{T\log(\frac{en}{k}})$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
